# OGFI ERP — Current Pending Implementation Plan

**As of:** July 26, 2026
**Status:** Active implementation register  
**Scope:** Production-readiness implementation remaining outside formal user UAT execution and final owner signoff

## 1. Purpose

This document is the working register for implementation that remains before OGFI ERP workspaces may be certified as production-ready. It separates shared platform work from workspace-specific completion and distinguishes required production controls from intentionally deferred future scope.

The delivery approach is:

1. Stabilize and verify the shared production foundation.
2. Complete one workspace at a time in dependency order.
3. Apply the full workspace completion gate before moving to the next workspace.
4. Stabilize Phase I and Phase 1.5 before certifying later phases for production.

Formal UAT execution, evidence collection with real users, owner signoff, and the final GO / NO-GO decision remain separate release activities.

### UAT acceleration priority — July 27, 2026

The fastest safe route to Phase I/UAT readiness is to close the shared disposable-PostgreSQL evidence gap before adding higher-risk writers. The runner must use a verified ephemeral, non-production target and execute the existing concurrent race, rollback, authorization/scope, segregation-of-duties, replay, and zero-adjacent-ledger suites for completed approval, cancellation, and terminal-source fences. An unavailable or misconfigured database is a hard **NO-GO** and must not be counted as passing evidence. After this foundation is evidenced, continue with bounded non-posting source fences, then complete visible workspace surfaces and the isolated Phase I UAT/recovery gates in dependency order. Approval routing remains disabled and C4 remains dormant until its separate policy and evidence gates are satisfied.

Runner safety validation now passes **15/15** lifecycle-contract tests, including target allowlisting, generated identity binding, credential scrubbing, marker attestation, and adversarial-target rejection. An exact runner invocation still fails closed with `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL_REQUIRED`; therefore no PostgreSQL race, rollback, ACL, or UAT evidence is credited yet.

## 3. Controlled Evidence Upload and Storage

Evidence capture will use a hybrid model:

- structured evidence notes;
- verified external references where policy permits them; and
- private file uploads where the workflow or risk policy requires an artifact.

Text alone must not satisfy high-risk evidence requirements unless an approved policy explicitly permits a verified external reference.

### Implemented foundation and pending activation

- The `DEC-0046` dedicated internal minimal storage broker is implemented for the same Hostinger VPS topology. Only the broker is configured to own the validated absolute private evidence bind mount and versioned AES-256-GCM key; hosted proof of that isolation remains required.
- Use separate mounts, encryption keys/versions, broker identities, scanner boundaries, credentials, and environment configuration for development, staging, and production.
- Use server-generated opaque non-repeating keys; keep tenant/company/source scope in PostgreSQL and do not encode original filenames or sensitive business data in storage keys.
- Store file metadata and source-record links in PostgreSQL; do not store file bytes in PostgreSQL.
- Enforce source-record authorization on upload, list, preview, download, archive, and replacement.
- Enforce location/department scope, confidential workforce access, and restricted-project membership where applicable.
- Validate configured size limits, allowed extensions, detected content type, and checksum.
- Upload into quarantine and prevent evidence use until malware scanning succeeds.
- Bounded upload and download streams are proxied through the authorized application and internal broker; filesystem paths, broker/scanner access, and permanent public URLs are not exposed.
- Audit upload, scan result, download, archive, replacement, denial, and retention actions.
- Preserve important evidence through archival/versioning rather than normal-user hard deletion.
- Application retention/legal-hold services and an administrative retention register, company quota reservations, upload leases/rate limits, disk high-water controls, and an evidence-root single-broker process lock are implemented. Recovery creation, isolated staging, and verification tools exist, but an independently recoverable encrypted backup, key escrow/custody, and hosted paired database/evidence restore proof remain activation blockers. Do not represent same-VPS filesystem storage as WORM or Object Lock.
- Provide one reusable attachment uploader/viewer for workspace adoption.
- Define the attachment requirement matrix by workflow, action, risk, value, and evidence purpose.
- Add cross-tenant, cross-company, cross-location, unauthorized-source, and restricted-project denial tests.

Direct web-mounted `local-private` storage remains suitable for local development and controlled demonstration only. The production design is the isolated broker contract in `DEC-0046`; confirmation of that architecture does not satisfy its implementation or activation gates.

### SPF-005 decision status — July 21, 2026

- `DEC-0046` supersedes `DEC-0045` and confirms a dedicated internal minimal broker on the same Hostinger VPS. Only the broker owns the absolute private evidence mount and versioned AES-256-GCM key; web, Caddy, and ClamAV have no evidence mount. The application proxies bounded streams, and the server issues opaque immutable keys/versions.
- PostgreSQL is authoritative for source-record authorization, idempotency, quota, quarantine, exact-version compare-and-set availability, retention/legal hold, and audit. ClamAV scans private streams through `INSTREAM`; only a conclusive `CLEAN` result for the exact version and accepted signature freshness becomes available. A bounded systemd timer handles reconciliation without detached post-response work, Redis, or a queue.
- Same-VPS filesystem storage is not WORM/Object Lock and retains root-compromise and same-disk-loss risks. SPF-005 is not production-ready. Activation remains blocked on actual VPS size/utilization and headroom, evidence quota/high-water values, encryption-key custody/recovery, Hostinger daily-backup entitlement/location/encryption/retention/restore granularity, approved RPO/RTO, retention/legal-hold policy, pinned ClamAV image/resources/signature freshness, implementation verification, and hosted paired database/evidence restore proof.
- External object storage is deferred until capacity, multi-host deployment, stronger RPO/RTO, legal WORM, or tenant scale triggers a new material decision and controlled copy-verify-cutover migration.

### SPF-006 implementation checkpoint — July 22, 2026

A direct-user first step creates one deterministic notification for the persisted assigned user; a role-scoped first step creates no personal notification. The existing permissioned manual reminder scan may create current-user reminders for eligible due or overdue work, but it is partial and its approval links remain unavailable. ### DEC-0107 My Tasks filter contract checkpoint — July 24, 2026

- Independent workflow and data/security reviews confirmed that the dashboard's required My Tasks filters cannot be shipped as client-side or post-merge filtering. - The current approval-engine checkpoint uses one transaction, scoped row locks, live authority revalidation, and compare-and-set transitions. All five initial procurement paths implement this contract, but role-scoped work has no complete actionable queue while `APPROVAL_ROUTING_V1_ENABLED=false`; the active step is authoritative, no parallel flag-off Inbox is authorized, and the existing current-user due/overdue reminder scan is only a partial non-actionable signal. Final approve, return, or reject writes one retry-safe outcome notification for the requester or responsible owner in the terminal-decision transaction. This bounded behavior does not enable broad external fanout.

## 4. Workspace Completion Sequence

After the shared baseline is stable, complete workspaces in this dependency order:

| Order | Workspace | Key completion focus | Status |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Overview and application shell | Default landing, navigation, scope context, role-aware summaries, responsive behavior, and safe drilldowns | In progress; `DEC-0053` action-first queue preview, explicit queue metadata, shell-control integrity, dashboard loading/error handling, bounded scoped source reads, `DEC-0054` feature-disabled Approval Inbox posture, closed Open PO/PR/Transfer/Adjustment/Wastage/Receiving Follow-up/Ledger Variance/Positive Stock drilldowns (`DEC-0055`–`DEC-0059`, `DEC-0069`, `DEC-0070`, `DEC-0231`), `DEC-0226` Branch Operations, `DEC-0227` Food Safety Reviews/Exceptions, `DEC-0228` Incident, and `DEC-0229` Maintenance profile destinations, mobile shell, and signed/paginated `My Tasks` coverage through controlled Stock Count, Branch Operations, Food Safety, Incident, and Maintenance adapters are implemented locally. `DEC-0066` adds the v2 priority/due/age cursor needed for risk- and due-aware sources. `DEC-0060` server-side blind-count redaction and reviewer-only Count Variance access are implemented; Count Variance remains inactive pending the `DEC-0061` immutable recount-attempt decision and its two open recovery/cutoff policies. `DEC-0071` implements the safe Food Cost fallback: no unresolved Overview projection or new exception reminder, with the source workspace retained. `DEC-0072` adds truthful per-source observation/partial states and bounded technical deadlines without inventing a freshness SLA, and `DEC-0230` applies the same bounded-settlement, retained-admission, and redacted-telemetry safety properties to My Tasks. Normalized approval cutover, Food Cost source-definition correction/reactivation, remaining browser/staging evidence, representative PostgreSQL reconciliation-plan/concurrency proof, hosted load/cancellation/recovery gates, and UAT remain pending |
| 2 | Administration | Companies, brands, locations, users, roles, scopes, policies, approval rules, security controls, and audit access | In progress; bounded registries/details and the `DEC-0225` company Approval Rule lifecycle are implemented, while disposable-PostgreSQL, responsive-browser, hosted deployment/recovery, UAT, and remaining visible-surface gates stay open |
| 3 | Master data | Suppliers, items, categories, units, locations, eligibility/deactivation, duplicate controls, and scoped access | In progress; `DEC-0239` locally protects Item creation/parent lifecycle concurrency, `DEC-0240` closes the greater-than-100-option Create Item dead end, and `DEC-0241` replaces broad selected-Item editing with a focused, version-checked name-correction TaskSheet while direct deactivation fails closed. `DEC-0242` locally closes the bounded Supplier Catalog confidentiality, shared-Supplier lifecycle serialization, stable paging, trusted recoverable task-state, and responsive same-result source contract. `DEC-0243` locally adds stateless option-catalog admission, independent debounced/cancellable selectors, exact edge zones, isolated aggregate health, and fail-closed production configuration. The flag stays disabled; resumable mapping-and-capability-bound backfill/drain, exact-candidate PostgreSQL authorization/concurrency, responsive browser, hosted recovery, UAT, and explicit activation remain open |

### Workspace 2 Administration implementation checkpoint — July 24, 2026

The existing service guard and DEC-0043 target-company rules are unchanged; roles, organization, audit, request, permission, and detail pagination remain separate follow-up slices.

The full web regression passes 1,291 tests across 120 files (301 skipped, 1 todo), the authorization manifest passes 20/20 after recording the new protected service surface, and the isolated production build passes. Responsive browser, disposable PostgreSQL boundary/count parity, and hosted gates remain open. Glossary, Users/Roles UI specification, administrator guide, release note, formal decision record, and decision index are aligned.

Responsive browser, disposable PostgreSQL authorization/no-mutation, full regression, production build, and hosted gates remain open. Users/Roles UI specification, administrator knowledge-base guidance, glossary, release note, formal decision record, and decision index are aligned.

Raw status, priority, and requested-date inputs cannot redefine a profile; normalized search is capped at 120 characters, searches visible metadata only, and may only narrow; missing, empty, duplicate, invalid, and stale profile context fails visibly before data access.
- Follow-up contains active `OPEN`, `IN_PROGRESS`, and `PENDING_VENDOR` tickets. Critical is an all-status historical priority lens. Pending Vendor is scoped oversight, not actor-assigned work. Overdue requires the dashboard's captured non-future `asOf` operating date and uses `targetDueAt` before that cutoff, `completedAt` null, and the active-status whitelist. This intentionally excludes cancelled and malformed completed/null-completion rows from active overdue work without rewriting stored history. The cutoff remains stable through navigation, but the destination reads current records and is not a historical snapshot. The four ticket populations may overlap and are not additive.
- Profile mode is read-only, hides create and ordinary export, rejects direct dashboard-profile export requests, uses a minimal visible-metadata triage projection, and preserves only canonical profile/search/page/applicable-cutoff return context through detail and actions. Source Incident ID is hidden from profile rows; any detail source link is permission-gated and independently reauthorized. Correction, completion, and cancellation independently reauthorize the live actor, status, and exact scope.
- Parent-led specialist deliberation accepted the bounded contract subject to exact nullable-brand scope, all-status Critical semantics, Pending Vendor oversight labeling, active-only captured-cutoff overdue semantics, visible-field search, minimal projection, fail-closed parameters, export rejection, independently authorized source links/actions, and parity/navigation tests. Requested Code Spark and exact GPT-5.4 models were unavailable, so the closest permitted GPT-5.6 specialists were used without relaxing gates.
- Assignment/reassignment authority, vendor-state transitions, terminal reopen, terminal-status/date integrity and recovery, and authoritative operating-timezone policy remain open under the core decision register. This checkpoint does not resolve or conceal them.
- Independent QA and Security re-reviews returned GO with no remaining Critical, High, or Medium finding after the profile source-Incident UUID was removed from row projections, exact permission-and-scope source reauthorization was added, all profile-reachable controls were raised to 44px, and the real company-level empty-brand session sentinel was normalized to database `null` in both Maintenance and the linked Incident destination. The source-disclosure matrix proves resolve-only access, no-Incident-authority redaction, out-of-scope redaction, and history redaction. Requested Code Spark and exact GPT-5.4 models were unavailable, so the closest permitted GPT-5.6 specialists were used without relaxing gates.
- Combined Maintenance, Incident regression, dashboard, export, and authorization-manifest coverage passes 167/167, including the 20/20 manifest gate. The complete non-database web suite passes 1,389 tests across 127 files (302 skipped, 1 todo); web and E2E typecheck, web lint, production build, secret review, and `git diff --check` pass. The projects/operations disposable-PostgreSQL runner was attempted and failed closed before database creation with `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL_REQUIRED`; no database execution credit is claimed. The coherent commit is the checkpoint commit containing this record; its exact SHA is verified after push. No authenticated desktop/tablet/mobile, hosted deployment/recovery, production-authenticated E2E, or UAT credit is claimed. Overview, Maintenance Management, Workspace 1, and Phase I remain in progress.

### Workspace 1 implementation checkpoint — July 23, 2026

- Confirmed `DEC-0053` after independent UX, correctness, and security review. The overview now places compact scope/freshness context before `Today’s work`, then compact KPI/supporting content. The earlier KPI-first wording in the Phase I dashboard screen specification was aligned with the higher-authority Dashboard Rules and the existing workspace audit.
- The server-derived approval and exception queue contracts now expose a bounded preview with deterministic priority ordering, location, owner, timing, and severity labels. Existing server-side permission and selected-scope behavior remain the data boundary; the UI does not supply scope, source type, record ID, or route values. The separate My Tasks workspace now provides exact totals and signed cross-source seek pagination for every enrolled source; totals and continuation are withheld whenever an enrolled source is unavailable. Enterprise-wide coverage remains intentionally incomplete because Approval Inbox, Count Variance, Food Cost, and sources without an approved action adapter are excluded.
- The Overview visibly labels the queue as a bounded priority preview and shows `shown of total` whenever its cap excludes matching records. Existing knowledge-base wording already describes `Today’s work` as a bounded preview, so no glossary or enablement change was required.
- A Playwright desktop/Pixel-7 check now verifies the Overview priority-preview disclosure, source-workspace headings, and no horizontal overflow. The test and web application typechecks/lint pass; execution remains pending because this environment does not provide `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL` for the required seeded disposable-database E2E harness.
- `DEC-0063` confirms the path from a bounded preview to a complete cross-source queue: Overview remains a preview, while a separate server-owned `My Tasks` workspace may enroll only source-owned, authorized, seek-paginated task adapters with one tested ordering/predicate contract. Exact totals are withheld whenever a registered source is unavailable; Approval, Count Variance, and Food Cost remain excluded under their existing gates. Implementation begins with source task-contract parity, not by increasing preview limits.
- Mobile navigation now uses a fixed four-action bar (`Overview`, the user's primary operational workspace, `Alerts`, and `More`) and a role-filtered workspace sheet. This replaces the prior multi-row fixed grid that could obscure the active workspace. Web typecheck and lint pass; seeded browser execution remains pending on `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL`. The glossary needs no change because no new user-facing term or control was introduced.
- Dashboard queue dates and due-state ranking now use the documented `Asia/Manila` operating day instead of UTC date slicing. A Manila-midnight regression test passes with the focused dashboard suite (19 tests), web typecheck, and lint. This corrects existing display/ranking behavior; it does not create a new user-facing term, workflow, or policy, so no glossary or knowledge-base edit is required.
- The first `DEC-0063` source-task adapter is now implemented for Wastage: it exposes only `SUBMITTED` reports to users with the existing review permission and `APPROVED` reports to users with the existing posting permission, with the same scoped predicate for exact count and bounded seek paging. Draft and returned reports are intentionally excluded because their current detail surface has no focused correction editor; exposing them as a task would falsely promise a completable action. Focused Wastage tests (17), web typecheck, and lint pass. This is a server-only queue foundation, so no glossary or knowledge-base update is required yet.
- Stock Adjustments now provide the second `DEC-0063` source-task adapter: only unposted `APPROVED` adjustments are returned, and only to users who already have the existing posting permission. The count and bounded seek page share that exact scoped predicate. Draft/correction work and approval work remain excluded until their authoritative focused surfaces are included; Approval Inbox remains the approval authority. Focused Stock Adjustment tests (18), web typecheck, and lint pass. This is a server-only queue foundation, so no glossary or knowledge-base update is required yet.
- Transfers now provide a third `DEC-0063` source-task adapter. It returns `REQUESTED` transfers only to dispatch-authorized users in the selected source location, and dispatched/partially received/disputed transfers only to receipt-authorized users in the selected destination location who are not the dispatcher. Dispute settlement remains excluded until its stricter independent-actor eligibility can be represented and parity-tested. The count and bounded seek page share this direction- and actor-aware predicate. Focused Transfer tests (22), web typecheck, and lint pass. This is a server-only queue foundation, so no glossary or knowledge-base update is required yet.
- The registered source adapters now share one deterministic cross-source cursor tuple: creation timestamp, fixed source rank, then source record ID. Same-timestamp tests prove that a later-ranked source remains eligible after a prior source page, while the current source advances only after its record ID; this prevents cross-source skips and duplicates. The first registry intentionally contains only high-priority, no-due-date task projections. Any source with different priority/due semantics must extend and test the common tuple before enrollment. Focused task-ordering and source tests (59), web typecheck, and lint pass. This internal contract introduces no user-facing term, so no glossary or knowledge-base update is required.
- The initial `My Tasks` workspace is now live in the Overview navigation. It server-merges only the enrolled requester-owned Purchase Request, Purchase Order, Transfer, Wastage, Stock Adjustment, and Receiving task adapters, reuses each authoritative service’s scoped action predicate, and opens the source record for the actual controlled action. The Purchase Order adapter returns only `DRAFT` orders to users with the existing submit permission and `APPROVED` orders to users with the existing supplier-send permission; it does not treat all open POs as tasks. Purchase Request submission now server-enforces that the draft’s recorded requester is the acting user before it creates routing, audit, or notification effects; the detail screen shows the true requester and explains the denied action. The `My Tasks` adapter returns only that user’s `DRAFT` requests with the existing submit permission. Submission on behalf remains explicitly open and is not inferred from read scope. The Receiving adapter returns only `DRAFT` receipts to users with the existing post permission; discrepancy review is excluded because it has no controlled resolution action. Its opaque HMAC-signed cursor is short-lived and bound to the current user, selected scope, permission set, and registered-source version; it cannot authorize a source action. Same-timestamp ordering remains gap-free across pages. A failed enrolled source is disclosed as unavailable and causes the total to be withheld rather than reported as zero. The page explicitly states its current source coverage, so it is an initial controlled queue rather than a claim of complete enterprise task coverage. Focused My Tasks, Purchase Request, Purchase Order, authentication, and source tests, web typecheck, and lint pass. Browser execution remains pending on `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL`. Knowledge-base and glossary now explain this distinction.
- `DEC-0064` enrolls Branch Operations with two exact source-owned task predicates: independently reviewable `SUBMITTED`/`MANAGER_REVIEW` checklists with complete actor lineage, and role-pooled `RETURNED` correction work for scoped creators. Final `REVIEWED`/`EXCEPTION_OPEN` close work remains excluded pending confirmed self-action/final-signoff policy. Count and page reuse the same tenant/company/optional-brand/location and action predicate; the adapter selects no lines, evidence, notes, or audit data. Direct review and return now also fail closed when opener or latest-submitter lineage is missing, and the Return control is shown only with the existing correction permission. Independent post-implementation QA accepted the remediation with no remaining blocker. Focused Branch Operations/My Tasks/task-ordering tests pass 22/22; web typecheck, lint, and `git diff --check` pass. Browser, disposable-database authorization, and hosted evidence remain pending under the workspace gate. The glossary and dashboard/My Tasks knowledge-base guide now distinguish personal work from role-pooled work.
- July 26 filter-parity correction: a correction-only Branch Operations or Food Safety actor no longer loses authorized pooled `RETURNED` work when selecting the matching My Tasks status. Both adapters use named permission predicates, review-only actors remain excluded from returned correction rows, and signed queue cursors advance to registry `v6`. - `DEC-0066` upgrades the signed My Tasks cursor to v2 and orders tasks by explicit critical/high/medium/low priority, dated-before-undated absolute due date, creation time, source rank, and record ID. Existing sources retain fixed `HIGH`/no-due semantics; v1 cursors fail safely and restart. Incident joins as one role-pooled resolution obligation per active record, using four bounded severity streams and exact tenant/company/null-aware-brand/location scope. - `DEC-0067` enrolls one role-pooled Maintenance completion obligation per eligible active ticket through four bounded native-priority streams. The adapter enforces exact tenant/company/null-aware-brand/location scope, `completedAt = null`, and known non-self reporter lineage for critical/high tickets; medium/low tickets retain the current scoped completion rule. Cancellation and ordinary correction remain destination-only and excluded from the queue. The destination now fails closed for missing high-risk reporter lineage, the UI mirrors that rule, Maintenance dashboard/detail/history/source-Incident reads use exact null-aware brand scope, and correction now consistently requires `restaurant.maintenance.correct`. Completion, cancellation, and correction use `updatedAt` compare-and-swap guards so a concurrent correction cannot bypass the risk decision or overwrite stale history. - `DEC-0068` enrolls exactly one assigned first-pass Stock Count obligation per eligible session: on-date `DRAFT` start, non-empty incomplete `IN_PROGRESS` entry, or non-empty complete `IN_PROGRESS` submission. It uses fixed `HIGH`/no-due ordering and excludes future starts, empty snapshots, `RECOUNT_REQUESTED`, submitted review/recount, cancellation, reviewed variance generation, and all Count Variance activation. Count start and all receiving, transfer, wastage, and adjustment posting/reversal paths share one complete, sorted, scoped Inventory Location lock set before source locks, so the balance snapshot/cutoff and movement freeze have one serialization boundary; the cutoff uses post-lock PostgreSQL wall-clock time. Independent reviews found and accepted remediation of entered-fact export leakage, review-control actor parity, transaction-start timestamp drift, and authorization-evidence overclaiming, with no remaining Critical/High code finding. - `DEC-0069` replaces the misleading `Receiving Variance` card with a closed `Receiving Follow-up` profile. One source-owned predicate includes clean `DRAFT`/`POSTING`, every `POSTED_WITH_DISCREPANCY`, and discrepancy-flagged `DRAFT`/`POSTING`/`POSTED`/`POSTED_WITH_DISCREPANCY`/`REVERSING` receipts in the selected receiving location while excluding terminal and future statuses. Dashboard count/candidates, authorized total, constrained GRN/PO/supplier search, 25-row server pages, inclusion reasons, and the header-only CSV export reuse that boundary. - `DEC-0070` closes the Ledger Variance drilldown through a dedicated `/inventory/reconciliation?dashboard=ledger-variance-v1` diagnostic profile. It requires both balance-view and ledger-view authority and uses one parameterized PostgreSQL statement to union balance-cache and movement keys for the exact tenant/company/selected active location, aggregate ledger deltas, round cache-minus-ledger variance to six decimals, and return only non-zero rows. The same query contract drives the dashboard count and three candidates, additive max-120 search, deterministic 25-row server pages, full filtered diagnostic export, and exact independently authorized ledger traces; no balance edit, Stock Adjustment, posting, approval, or reversal action is exposed. Trust-gate `block` suppresses the numeric dashboard value while retaining a visibly untrusted diagnostic path for authorized investigation. Independent Workflow, Security, and UX review first rejected the exact trace because it was ledger-only, silently capped at 100 movements, did not identify resolved keys, and was misclassified in generated authorization evidence. Remediation now requires both permissions for exact traces, rejects trace filters from the generic ledger reader, provides deterministic 50-row trace pages with exact totals and preserved return context, displays current cache/ledger/variance quantities plus a resolved/stale warning, classifies reconciliation surfaces as the explicit permission conjunction, and adds an executable export boundary matrix for neither, either permission alone, and both. The UX re-review additionally identified and closed sub-44px controls and missing route states: affected controls and paging now have 44px minimum targets, and both reconciliation and ledger routes provide scoped loading skeletons plus user-safe retry boundaries. Security and static UX re-reviews returned GO. The requested Spark/GPT-5.4 models were unavailable, so the closest permitted GPT-5.6 specialist roles were used and recorded. The complete non-database web unit suite passes, including 23 Inventory service tests, 12 reconciliation/ledger route UI tests, and the 20-test authorization manifest; web typecheck, lint, and production build pass. The new disposable-PostgreSQL route boundary case is implemented but remains locally unexecuted until a disposable database is available. Integration also corrected invalid dashboard-profile CSV requests so Purchase Order, Purchase Request, Transfer, Wastage, Adjustment, and Receiving exports fail closed through shared no-store validation responses instead of returning generic data. Workspace 1 and Inventory remain in progress because representative PostgreSQL `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)`, concurrent-posting snapshot evidence, route-matrix execution, export-volume policy, authenticated responsive-browser evidence, and hosted gates remain pending. Glossary, permission/reporting/dashboard/Inventory specifications, user guide, release note, decision record, and changelog are aligned.
- `DEC-0071` implements the `DEC-0062` safe fallback instead of leaving known-ambiguous Food Cost signals live. Overview no longer imports or calls the Food Cost analysis, and it emits no Food Cost card, KPI, exception candidate, unavailable-source item, source-health claim, readiness badge, or aggregate contribution. Authorized recipe/cost users retain only neutral `Source workspace` navigation to the independently authorized `/recipes/analysis` route. Restaurant Operations scans no longer read Food Cost, create or refresh `FOOD_COST_EXCEPTION`, or accept recipe-only eligibility; Branch Operations, Food Safety, Incident, and Maintenance reminders remain enrolled. Existing Food Cost reminders remain stored, visible, readable, and manually archivable with a non-mutating legacy-definition warning, and they do not contribute to the page's current actionable count. Independent review exposed and drove remediation of an existing tenant-only notification boundary: list, unread count, read, and archive now share an exact tenant/recipient/selected-company and null-or-selected-location predicate, require an active selected location and a live effective company-or-location assignment, hide company-less rows, and silently deny forged or revoked-scope mutations. Notification list/count are now explicitly high-risk disclosures in the authorization manifest, and all four entrypoints have executable live-context bindings. The disposable-PostgreSQL matrix covers cross-company/location/null-company/forged/revoked cases, company-wide rows, immutable legacy Food Cost history, and two authorized idempotent scans yielding exactly the six non-Food-Cost reminders; it is implemented but locally unexecuted because `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL` is unavailable. Final independent QA and Security re-reviews returned GO with no remaining Critical/High finding. The requested Spark/GPT-5.4 models were unavailable, so GPT-5.6 reporting, workflow, UX, backend, QA, and security fallbacks were used and recorded. Workspace 1 remains in progress because a fresh production-build result, `DEC-0062` definition/reactivation work, normalized approval cutover, authenticated responsive-browser proof, disposable-PostgreSQL execution, and hosted release/recovery gates remain pending. Decision record, security/permission/reporting/UI/UAT sources, glossary, user guidance, release note, and changelog are aligned.
- The application shell no longer displays a decorative search affordance. Its notification control now opens the existing Notification Center. The dashboard adds layout-preserving loading and safe retry error states.
- Seven source services now provide dashboard-specific, server-authorized, selected-scope reads. Purchase Requests, Receiving, Transfers, Stock Counts, Wastage, and Stock Adjustments return an exact scoped count and no more than eight narrow header/exception candidates; they do not expose line, comment, audit, or evidence collections. Purchase Orders use scoped count/amount aggregates, a narrow fulfillment-line projection needed for the existing exact open/received-value calculation, and no more than eight overdue header candidates instead of materializing their related workspace records. Transfer candidates put `DISPUTED` items ahead of ordinary follow-up items before the bounded cap. The source workspace still reauthorizes every opened record.
- Branch Operations now follows the same dashboard-read boundary: Overview uses selected-scope status/exception aggregates, a critical-exception aggregate, and no more than three narrow review candidates plus three narrow exception candidates. The full checklist/line query remains confined to the authoritative Branch Operations workspace.
- Food Safety now follows the same dashboard-read boundary: Overview uses selected-scope log/reading/exception aggregates and no more than three narrow review candidates plus three narrow exception candidates. Full safety-log and reading detail remains confined to the authoritative Food Safety workspace.
- Incidents now follow the same dashboard-read boundary: Overview uses selected-scope status/severity/overdue aggregates and no more than three narrow follow-up candidates. Full incident detail and correction/evidence context remains confined to the authoritative Incident workspace.
- Maintenance now follows the same dashboard-read boundary: Overview uses selected-scope status/priority/overdue/downtime aggregates and no more than three narrow follow-up candidates. Full maintenance ticket detail, history, and operational evidence remains confined to the authoritative Maintenance workspace.
- `DEC-0062` leaves Food Cost dashboard-read replacement open. Independent reporting review found mixed default-date source data, missing ledger valuation represented as zero, and an `AWAITING_ACTUALS` definition mismatch. Do not add a bounded Food Cost dashboard projection or call the current analytical result equivalent until those source definitions are confirmed, corrected, and parity-tested; hiding the summary is the safe fallback.
- `DEC-0054` confirms the feature-disabled Approval Inbox posture. While normalized approval routing remains deliberately disabled, Overview no longer calls the unbounded legacy approval list or substitutes a direct approval query. It explicitly directs authorized users to the controlled Approval Inbox and does not represent its unavailable preview as zero assignments, a clear indicator, or an alert count. The eventual bounded approval adapter remains conditional on approved normalized-routing cutover and a minimal source-authorized projection.
- `DEC-0072` replaces eager shared dashboard-source mutation with lazy, authorized descriptors whose reads return immutable patches. Every attempted authorized source settles independently as available or unavailable with an ISO-UTC check time; unauthorized and suppressed sources are omitted. The response records assembly time, preserves a documented native `Data as of` only for sources that supply one, and never calls observation time fresh, stale, live, complete, or real-time. A deployment-configurable technical deadline defaults to 2,500 ms and rejects values above 3,000 ms; late results cannot change the returned response. A process-wide admission controller defaults to 32 and caps at 64 executing source reads, retains timed-out slots until the underlying work actually settles, and emits safe timeout/saturation/late-settlement/assembly telemetry. Composite queue contracts expose contributor provenance and make the complete total unavailable when any contributor fails, while the UI labels remaining rows as a partial view and links generically to the authoritative workspace. Trust-policy degradation fails closed for reconciliation values, and the feature-disabled Approval preview is explicitly unavailable. The dashboard Inventory Balance input is now a bounded aggregate rather than an unpaginated full-row list, with an executable disposable-PostgreSQL aggregate plus public-dashboard scope/value case registered under the procurement/inventory authorization gate. Initial Architecture and Security review found the trust, availability, telemetry, database-evidence, and unbounded-late-work gaps; final re-reviews accepted their remediation with no remaining Critical/High finding. The complete non-database web suite passes 1,233 tests, including 54 focused dashboard/inventory tests and 8 dashboard UI tests; web typecheck, lint, the 20/20 authorization manifest, isolated-output production build, and `git diff --check` pass. The PostgreSQL case is implemented but locally unexecuted until `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL` is available. Workspace 1 remains in progress pending authenticated responsive-browser evidence, database-side cancellation/query-plan proof, staging fault injection against the four-second NFR, and the existing approval/Food Cost/hosted gates.
- `DEC-0055` confirms closed, source-owned dashboard destinations. The first activated destination is `Open POs`: the dashboard card, selected-scope count, server-paginated list, and CSV export share the exact `DRAFT`, `PENDING_APPROVAL`, `APPROVED`, `ISSUED`, `AMENDMENT_PENDING`, and `PARTIALLY_RECEIVED` lifecycle set. The destination accepts no client scope, record, status, or generic filter override; the target list, export, record view, and actions reauthorize the current session independently. Invalid dashboard-profile values do not activate a profile.
- `DEC-0056` adds the second closed, source-owned destination: `Open PRs`. Its card, selected-scope count, server-paginated list, and CSV export share the exact `DRAFT`, `PENDING_APPROVAL`, `APPROVED`, and `RETURNED` lifecycle set. The profile ignores raw status and search values, accepts no client scope or record authority, and each source operation reauthorizes the current session independently. Its user-facing wording explicitly includes approved requests.
- `DEC-0057` adds `Transfer Follow-up` as the third closed, source-owned destination. Its card, selected-scope count, server-paginated list, and CSV export share exactly `REQUESTED`, `DISPATCHED`, `PARTIALLY_RECEIVED`, and `DISPUTED` transfers where the selected location is the source or destination. The profile ignores raw tab/status/scope/filter overrides and grants no dispatch, receipt, settlement, or inventory capability; record actions independently reauthorize the current session.
- `DEC-0058` adds `Adjustment Exceptions` as a closed, source-owned destination. Its count, server-paginated list, and CSV export share exactly `PENDING_APPROVAL`, `APPROVED`, `POSTING`, and `RETURNED` records in the selected inventory location. It is read-only and preserves source authorization, no-self-approval, MFA, immutable-ledger, posting, and reversal controls.
- `DEC-0059` adds `Wastage Exceptions` as a closed, source-owned destination, sharing the selected inventory location's existing status-or-missing-required-evidence exception predicate across its count, server-paginated list, and CSV export. It remains read-only and preserves evidence policy, approval, audit, idempotent immutable posting, and reversal controls. Count Variance remains deliberately inactive: independent workflow and security review found that non-review users can currently derive frozen system quantity from the returned/displayed variance and that recount overwrites earlier evidence. Do not activate a variance KPI, queue, drilldown, or sensitive export until server-side redaction, review-only access, immutable recount history, and controlled adjustment recovery are implemented and verified.
- `DEC-0060` closes the immediate blind-count disclosure: non-review count reads and UI now redact system/variance/reviewer-derived fields, and Count Variance dashboard reads are review-permission gated. Count Variance remains inactive pending immutable recount history and controlled adjustment recovery; this security fix does not change count posting, adjustment generation, approval, or ledger behavior.
- Focused dashboard/adapter tests (including the closed PO/PR/Transfer profiles), web lint, web typecheck, the authorization-manifest gate (20), the isolated production build, release-tool self-test, and `git diff --check` pass. The refreshed authorization baseline classifies the new Branch Operations, Food Safety, Incident, and Maintenance dashboard reads as standard server-scoped workspace reads. ### DEC-0230 My Tasks bounded source settlement — July 26, 2026

Timeout, saturation, and exception name the affected source as unavailable; healthy-source rows remain visible, while the total and continuation cursor are withheld so recovered work cannot be skipped or represented as zero.

### DEC-0239 Master Data Item Category/UOM lifecycle locks — July 26, 2026

- The Master Data production-readiness review is in progress. `DEC-0239` closes the local source race in which Item creation and concurrent Item Category or UOM deactivation could independently validate and both commit. Creation now locks and revalidates the exact scoped Category followed by sorted distinct base/purchase/issue UOMs inside the transaction. Category and UOM deactivation lock the same scoped parent before counting active dependent Items and changing status. The serialized contract permits only one safe ordering: creation wins and deactivation rejects its active dependent, or deactivation wins and creation rejects the inactive parent. `DEC-0241` later narrowed current Item editing to name-only correction, so material edits do not participate in this parent lifecycle matrix. Existing tenant/company authorization, duplicate/base-UOM-history controls for future governed edits, lifecycle states, stable errors, atomic audit, and no-hard-delete behavior remain unchanged.
- Parent-led deliberation began with Product favoring the greater-than-100-option composer, UX favoring a focused TaskSheet, and Security identifying the lifecycle race as Blocking. The challenge round applied the data-integrity hard gate and reached consensus on the race-first correction. The option composer and TaskSheet remain separate visible-surface completion work. No schema, migration, permission, workflow, inventory, purchasing, reporting, export, or user-authority change is introduced.
- Focused Item coverage passes 10/10; the complete non-database web suite passes 1,424 tests with 305 skipped and one existing TODO across 129 passed/11 skipped files. Web typecheck/lint, E2E typecheck, production build, the 20/20 authorization manifest, secret review, and `git diff --check` pass. Final Product, Security, and UX reviews each return **GO** C0/H0/M0/L0 for this local source checkpoint. The current executable disposable-PostgreSQL matrix is authored and registered in the procurement/inventory runner: four tests cover Item creation against Category and base/purchase/issue UOM deactivation, and each test runs both winner orders for eight races. The matrix requires distinct backend PIDs, observes real waits through `pg_blocking_pids`, checks stable loser errors, and asserts the final invariant plus atomic source/audit outcomes. It is discovered and skips safely without the integration sentinel. The runner was attempted but still fails closed before database creation with `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL_REQUIRED`, so no PostgreSQL or two-connection race execution credit is claimed. ### DEC-0241 Master Data controlled Item correction TaskSheet — July 26, 2026

- Parent-confirmed `DEC-0241` replaces the selected existing-Item inline edit/deactivation block with a URL-selected workspace TaskSheet. For an `ACTIVE` Item, Item Name is the only writable business field and a correction reason is required. Item code, Category, item type, base/purchase/issue UOM, inventory/expiry/lot tracking, receiving inspection, and lifecycle state are read-only. The trusted-origin action delegates to a service that locks the exact tenant/company Item row, requires `ACTIVE`, compares `expectedUpdatedAt` under the lock, rejects no-op and forged material changes, and commits only the name plus its reason-bearing audit event atomically. Rejected stale, inactive, absent/foreign, no-op, or material-change attempts create no source or audit mutation.
- Direct Item deactivation now fails closed with `ITEM_DEACTIVATION_GOVERNANCE_REQUIRED`. The TaskSheet truthfully shows `Deactivate Item` as disabled and explains that Warehouse/Purchasing review, on-hand-stock and open procurement/inventory transaction checks, and a replacement plan where applicable are not implemented; no request is recorded or implied. Non-active Items are retained read-only, malformed/missing/cross-company selections share a generic unavailable state, close preserves register query/status/page context, and the exact Item audit handoff opens the authoritative Admin Audit view. No schema, migration, permission, approval route, inventory posting, report, export, or new lifecycle behavior was introduced.
- Product, Security, and UX deliberation applied the governance hard gate: the earlier broad material edit and direct-deactivation paths could not remain without the documented impact review and lifecycle controls. Requested Code Spark and exact GPT-5.4 models were unavailable, so the closest permitted GPT-5.6 role fallbacks were used without relaxing any hard gate. A disposable-PostgreSQL specification is authored and registered with 14 discovered cases: one two-writer CAS race, stale, inactive, foreign-company, direct-deactivation fail-closed, and nine material-forgery cases. The authenticated milestone browser specification is also updated to open the selected Item TaskSheet and assert its disabled, no-request deactivation state. Both are authored only: the runner attempt fails closed before database creation with `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL_REQUIRED`, so neither database nor browser execution credit is claimed. Authenticated responsive browser, hosted recovery/deployment, governed material-change/deactivation/reactivation workflows, Supplier catalog mobile layout, option-endpoint operational controls, role-policy reconciliation, UAT, and final Master Data production-readiness evidence remain open. Master Data, Workspace 3, and Phase I remain incomplete and **NO-GO**.
- Final focused Item coverage passes 20/20. The complete non-database web suite passes 1,434 tests with 305 skipped and one existing TODO across 130 passed/11 skipped files. Web typecheck/lint, E2E typecheck, production build, the regenerated 20/20 authorization manifest, secret review, Playwright discovery of 18 selected-Item cases, and diff hygiene pass. Final Product, Security, and UX reviews each return **GO** C0/H0/M0/L0 for this bounded local source checkpoint. The exact disposable-PostgreSQL and authenticated E2E runners were retried and both failed closed with `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL_REQUIRED`; no external execution credit is claimed.

### DEC-0242 Master Data Supplier Catalog confidentiality and lifecycle integrity — July 26, 2026

- Parent-confirmed `DEC-0242` adds the sensitive `purchasing.supplier_confidential.view` permission as additional clearance, not standalone Supplier authority. Ordinary reads still require existing Supplier workspace access and exact selected-company scope; writes still require `core.administer` plus company `MANAGE`. Without clearance, Supplier payment terms and latest Supplier Item reference-price/currency/effective metadata must be omitted before UI projection and represented only as `Restricted`. Unauthorized crafted nonblank writes must fail closed. `CONFIGURED_ADMIN` receives no default or recommended grant; only the existing superuser all-permission seed behavior may include it.
- The checkpoint also requires exact Supplier/link binding; scoped `ACTIVE` row locks and transactional compare-and-swap for Supplier and Supplier Item link deactivation with one winner/one audit; deterministic business ordering plus stable ID tie-breakers; exact counts and server-clamped bounded pages; and one server result set rendered as a desktop table or mobile cards. Catalog URL, filters, option/link pagers, selected task context, ranges, and true/filtered empty states must persist. Create/deactivate work uses focused task modes with 44-pixel-minimum targets and no horizontal mobile scrolling. No Supplier eligibility, accreditation, Purchase Order, receiving, inventory, payment, report, or export behavior changes.
- Local implementation now omits confidential fields at query level without live clearance; ignores URL-forged success; returns safe create/deactivate results through trusted Server Action state; retains non-confidential tab-scoped drafts and live sensitive fields on recoverable error without persisting confidential values to browser storage; preserves selected Item/UOM lookup context; and provides explicit pending, success, focus, inactive, true-empty, and filtered-empty behavior. Supplier deactivation, accreditation updates, link creation, and link deactivation serialize through the exact active Supplier row; strict effective-date validation rejects impossible calendar dates. The permission catalog row must remain on rollback to preserve role links; application rollback must fail closed and never restore broad exposure.
- Focused Supplier/action-feedback/permission/TaskSheet coverage passes 30/30. The complete non-database web suite passes 1,444 tests with 305 skipped and one existing TODO across 131 passed/11 skipped files. Web and database typechecks, web lint, E2E typecheck, production build, the 20/20 authorization manifest, secret review, the 135-migration safety inventory with the new exact-hash row held `PENDING`, Playwright discovery of 12 logical Supplier cases across 24 desktop/mobile project cases, and diff hygiene pass. The registered disposable-PostgreSQL specification authors 13 cases for both read projections, standalone/live/revoked clearance, crafted writes, exact binding, inactive-parent read-only behavior, 21-row tied pagination, duplicate deactivation, and three deterministic cross-writer lifecycle cases. The latter force both winner orders for accreditation, link creation, and link deactivation against Supplier deactivation—six exact race executions with observed row-lock waiting and atomic source/audit invariants.
- Product, Security, and UX/QA first-round reviews found and drove remediation of forgeable success, recoverable-error draft loss, dirty-focus jumps, stale writer races, invalid dates, inactive-parent mutation, confidential browser persistence, mobile-only selector regressions, lookup-selection loss, and mobile task placement. Final bounded-source Security and UX/QA reviews return **GO** C0/H0/M0/L0; final Product review returns **GO** C0/H0/M0/L1 after verifying all six forced winner orders. Its non-blocking Low recommends future full audit `entityType` and before/after-payload assertions; no bounded local source blocker remains. External PostgreSQL and authenticated browser runners were retried and failed closed before database creation with `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL_REQUIRED`, so no database, query-plan, concurrency, browser, migration deployment, hosted recovery, or UAT execution credit is claimed. Requested Code Spark and exact GPT-5.4 models were unavailable, so the closest permitted GPT-5.6 role fallbacks were used without relaxing hard gates. Supplier Master Data, Workspace 3, and Phase I remain incomplete and **NO-GO**.
- Glossary assessment: `Supplier confidential access` is a new reusable permission concept. The user-facing glossary, Supplier administration article, release notes, and administrator training guidance now document the implemented restricted-state, grant, draft-recovery, and lifecycle behavior without broadening policy.

## 5. Workspace Completion Gate

Every workspace must satisfy all applicable items before it is marked complete:

- All visible tabs, panels, actions, and navigation destinations work or clearly explain their intentional read-only/disabled state.
- Create, edit, submit, approve, post, receive, dispatch, complete, cancel, archive, reverse, export, and configuration actions work where the UI implies them.
- Server-side tenant and scope authorization is enforced; UI hiding is not treated as security.
- Valid transitions succeed and invalid transitions are rejected with stable, user-safe errors.
- Financial and inventory segregation-of-duties rules, including no self-approval, are enforced.
- Retried posting actions are idempotent and concurrency-sensitive actions detect stale versions.
- Important changes preserve audit/activity history and do not hard-delete controlled records.
- Required reasons, evidence, attachments, and independent review are policy-driven and enforced.
- Lists provide the required search, filters, pagination, export, and mobile presentation.
- Empty, loading, error, denied, returned, rejected, conflict, and retry states are usable.
- Desktop, tablet, and mobile task flows are verified.
- Relevant unit, integration, access-control, and focused E2E tests pass.
- Workflow, permission, data, UI, knowledge-base, release-note, and training documentation impacts are assessed and updated where behavior changed.

## 6. Phase I and Phase 1.5 Deferred Controlled Transitions

The following remain pending implementation or require a separate confirmed release slice. They must not be represented as available merely because adjacent foundations exist:

- full post-receiving PO amendment for supplier, location, lines, substitutions, and payment terms;
- partial receiving-line reversal;
- transfer dispatch reversal and automated replacement/financial settlement;
- backdated operational corrections outside approved controlled paths;
- opening-balance and inventory cutover execution after the final cutover policy is confirmed;
- automated notification scheduling and external email/chat/SMS delivery;
- time-limited attachment links where direct signed URLs are adopted;
- formal PDF summaries where required;
- broader task dependency enforcement, drag/drop concurrency, and automation beyond the approved Phase 1.5 boundary.
- Phase 1.5 Project Board still has implementation blockers before its workspace gate: the visible board needs the documented project context header, linked-record/assignee indicators, complete status controls, mobile filtered-list mode, and server pagination; task-key allocation must replace the concurrent `count + 1` pattern; project/task dates must render in the company or user timezone; and the enabled task-transition policy needs an explicit lifecycle matrix. These are recorded for the later Projects & Implementation Tracker workspace and must not be hidden behind the current Phase I stabilization work.
- The same Phase 1.5 audit found that `/projects` reports all active members and open risks but renders only `members.slice(0, 10)` and `risks.slice(0, 8)` from unbounded service reads. Replace those truncations with server-owned, scoped pagination and truthful page/empty/error states before the project workspace can pass SPF-008.
- Administration remains the next Phase I workspace after Overview stabilization. Its previously identified unbounded/hard-capped users, roles, organization, audit, request, and permission reads have received bounded URL-backed contracts and focused visible-state hardening through the DEC-0162–DEC-0218 checkpoints. Administration is still not complete: disposable PostgreSQL no-query/isolation/query-plan evidence, responsive browser/mobile verification, hosted recovery/deployment, production-authenticated E2E, and UAT remain open, and no server guard may be relaxed.

## 7. Later-Phase Boundaries

### Phase 2 — Restaurant Operations and Food Cost

Pending production work includes full regression, mobile operational verification, trusted sales/POS source validation, attachment adoption, training impact, and resolution of any formula or workflow defects found during the workspace pass. Recursive sub-recipe flattening and bulk mutation remain deferred unless separately approved.

### Phase 3 — Finance and Workforce

The current implementation is a controlled foundation. Production implementation remains pending for supplier-credit application, AP settlement, payment execution, bank effects/integration, official accounting consequences, cash-advance and petty-cash settlement, budget hard-block rollout/backfill, broader reconciliation exceptions, production close resolution, full workforce transfer approval, and production document retention.

Phase 3 must not be presented as the official accounting book of record until these controls and the required finance-owner validations are complete.

### Phase 4 — Expansion Projects

The workspace foundation remains subject to the shared attachment/storage, authorization, release-evidence, and workspace completion gates. Contractor portals, public links, advanced Gantt calculations, workload balancing, and custom automation builders remain intentionally out of scope unless separately approved.

## 8. Production Configuration Still Required

- Approval thresholds and authorized approvers
- Emergency-purchase ceiling and post-review deadline
- Evidence and attachment requirements by workflow, action, value, and risk
- Lot/expiry-controlled categories
- Stock-count frequency by location/category
- Opening inventory date and valuation method
- Supplier accreditation and blocked-supplier rules
- Retention, privacy, backup, and restore ownership
- Production evidence-storage provider, limits, scanning, and recovery policy
- Monitoring, incident response, and hypercare ownership

### Workspace 3 governed Item lifecycle implementation lock — July 27, 2026
- Parent-led independent Product/Branch Operations, Architecture/Data, Security, and QA/Release deliberation rejected direct material-change/deactivation/reactivation actions and approval attached to mutable Item state. ### DEC-0245 durable Approval backfill and drain orchestration checkpoint — July 27, 2026

- Parent-led Architecture, Database, Security, and QA deliberation confirmed one authoritative tenant/company PostgreSQL run with immutable routing/capability/release binding, database-time lease and monotonic fencing, one bounded Serializable APPLY page per invocation, and atomic append-only batch/blocker/checkpoint evidence.
- The feature-disabled source checkpoint contains true read-only exact-tenant/company `DRY_RUN`; explicit durable `START` and `RESUME`; exact-request replay; terminal-candidate counts; bounded machine-safe failure reasons; stable `CONTINUE`, `BLOCKED`, `RETRYABLE`, `INCOMPATIBLE`, and `BARRIER_REQUIRED` results; routing-enabled APPLY/STOP denial; and a scoped, contract-bound, fenced `STOP` that returns `STOPPED`, appends and links an exact same-transaction audit, terminates the run, clears its lease, and preserves prior evidence. ### Finance Close cancellation lifecycle review — July 27, 2026 (NO-GO)

- Parent-led Architecture, Security, and QA challenge rejected an isolated cancellation barrier/CAS patch. `cancelPeriodCloseRun` only admits mutable readiness states, while sensitive lock/reopen approval is admitted only from `CLOSED`; therefore pending sensitive approval is not a reachable legitimate cancellation state. More importantly, readiness recalculation, completion/soft-close, checklist, and exception writers do not share a source-lock/version fence, so cancellation alone could still be overwritten by stale work or allow post-cancel child mutation.
- The safe future slice is coupled lifecycle fencing: shared barrier → locked FinanceCloseRun → AccountingPeriod read lock → explicit incoherence/graph policy → source CAS, together with matching parent locks/version predicates for readiness, completion, checklist, and exception writers. It must also define durable cancellation replay identity and remain strictly non-mutating to period, readiness, checklist, exception, AP, payment, bank, journal, and inventory state. No code, schema, UI, or user-facing policy changed in this review; no glossary/KB/training update is required.

### Inventory Transfer receipt writer review — July 27, 2026 (NO-GO)

- Independent architecture review (permitted GPT-5.4-mini fallback) and Security challenge (permitted GPT-5.6 fallback; requested Spark/GPT-5.4 unavailable) reject enabling the current receipt writer. It preloads stale transfer/line facts, lacks authoritative header/line/receipt locks and CAS, has no durable receipt idempotency key/request hash, lacks transaction-time privileged MFA, and does not yet share a proven lock order with reversal. A hardened event-backed receipt slice is the only acceptable next implementation: additive receipt idempotency schema/UI/hash, exact replay/conflict handling, source/line/receipt CAS, reversal lock-order alignment, accepted-only `TRANSFER_IN`, rollback/recovery, and PostgreSQL race evidence. Until that design and migration are approved, receipt remains deferred and Workspace 4 / Phase I remains **NO-GO**.
- Additive receipt identity foundation is now authored: nullable `InventoryTransferReceipt.idempotencyKey` (`VARCHAR(200)`) and `idempotencyRequestHash` (`CHAR(64)`) with tenant/company/key uniqueness, preserving legacy null rows. Prisma format/validate and database typecheck pass. The migration has not been deployed to PostgreSQL because no disposable administrator URL is configured; receipt service/UI enforcement, replay handling, MFA, reversal alignment, and all database evidence remain open.
- Receipt request identity contract is partially wired: receive requests now require a bounded `idempotencyKey`, the receive form emits a stable-per-render `ui:transfer-receipt:<UUID>` key, and `hashInventoryTransferReceiptRequest` canonicalizes actor, destination scope, transfer, notes, authoritative inventory-location lineage, sorted line IDs, six-decimal quantities, and discrepancy evidence into a SHA-256 digest. Focused transfer coverage passes **26/26** and repository typecheck passes. The service still does not persist or enforce the hash/key transactionally; receipt remains **NO-GO** pending authoritative replay/CAS/MFA and PostgreSQL evidence.
- Receipt transaction hardening is now partially implemented: the writer locks the initial inventory-location set, transfer header, and ordered lines; rehydrates authoritative transfer/line/location scope; checks active locations and dispatcher separation; performs transaction-time privileged MFA; persists the idempotency key/hash; rejects hash conflicts and in-progress reuse; permits exact posted/reversed replay; uses authoritative line quantities and tenant/company CAS predicates; and CASes the parent status with the locked `updatedAt`. Focused transfer coverage passes **27/27**, repository typecheck and lint pass. Unique-key race handling, reversal lock-order alignment, rollback/replay PostgreSQL evidence, and production activation remain open; receipt and Workspace 4 / Phase I stay **NO-GO**.
- Reversal now begins adopting the receipt lock-order contract: it locks the posting inventory locations first, then the scoped transfer header and ordered lines, rehydrates the authoritative receipt/transfer graph, and fails closed if the location set or destination scope changed before locking. Repository typecheck and focused transfer coverage remain green (**27/27**). Transaction-time reversal MFA, receipt/header CAS version binding, unique-key race evidence, and full reversal-vs-receive PostgreSQL proof remain open; this is not a completion claim.
- Reversal privileged MFA is now evaluated inside the authoritative transaction after the location/header/line locks and receipt reload, using the locked receipt ID and transaction client. Focused transfer coverage remains **27/27** and repository typecheck passes. Receipt/header CAS version binding, unique-key race handling, rollback/replay PostgreSQL evidence, and hosted UAT gates remain open; receipt remains **NO-GO**.
- Reversal receipt claiming now binds the `POSTED`/unreversed transition to the authoritative receipt `updatedAt`, preserving stale-writer rejection after the locked reload. Focused transfer coverage remains **27/27**; PostgreSQL contention and rollback proof are still unavailable without the disposable administrator URL.
- Receipt creation now classifies the tenant/company/idempotency-key unique constraint and returns the stable `TRANSFER_RECEIPT_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` code instead of leaking a database error. On a unique race, it performs a fresh scoped lookup and replays only when the stored canonical hash matches and the raced receipt is terminal (`POSTED`/`REVERSED`); otherwise it fails closed. Focused transfer coverage remains **27/27** and repository typecheck passes. Disposable PostgreSQL contention/rollback evidence and hosted UAT gates remain open; receipt remains **NO-GO**.
- Reversal now CASes the parent transfer status update against the locked transfer status and `updatedAt`, with exact tenant/company/destination scope predicates, after counter-movement and line-rollup work. Focused transfer coverage remains **27/27** and repository typecheck passes. Disposable PostgreSQL reversal-vs-receive contention, rollback, and balance-neutrality evidence remain open.
- Receipt idempotency race classification now also recognizes PostgreSQL `P2010`/`23505` errors when the named receipt idempotency index is involved, while unrelated database errors continue to propagate. Focused transfer coverage remains **27/27**; disposable PostgreSQL proof is still required.
- Disposable PostgreSQL lifecycle safety verification passes **15/15** (`pnpm test:disposable-db-lifecycle`), including explicit administrator-URL requirement and cross-run/target isolation guards. No database-backed receipt/race suite was executed because `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL` remains unset.
- Receive replay checks now run before rejecting an already-`RECEIVED` parent status, so a matching terminal idempotency key can replay after completion while a reused/mismatched key still fails closed. No posting behavior is enabled by this ordering correction; focused transfer coverage remains **27/27**.

- Parent-led Architecture, Security, and QA review selected a source-control-only hardening slice.
